# Parity tracker

Target: reproduce player-visible workflows using independent code and an owner-controlled backend. The installed PLAYGG.RO client is the visual reference. A feature is not complete until its flow is tested in this project.

## Verified release baseline — PR272

## Native comparison baseline — PR259, surface corrected PR260

The native window and isolated installed PR257 fixture were both captured at1200×963. The comparison confirms a legacy preview-background mismatch and incorrect center-label contract, and identifies remaining promotion/metadata/typography/window semantics. Native child activation occurred despite a background launch request; original was minimized and gracefully quit after read-only navigation. No real-money game action was sent. At capture time product code was PR257;PR260/PR262 resolve texture,label and plate contracts. [Findings and exact evidence scope](docs/rea/native-table-comparison.md). Raw images stay local; do not relaunch the native client in background-only work.

## Historical release baseline — PR260

PR260 merged `dda375efc44fd7fe67c6206033bbec1b144dc560`; tested source `15024c8eb88d136a1f3d44eb2220f42f74e2e408`.

The ordinary local Hold’em Table preview now uses the original `lobby_preview0000` texture (223 × 351), decoded-RGBA verified against the installed atlas. TableViewComponent selects this texture and defaults to scale 1.08; HoldemTablePreview does not override it. The CSS paints 240.84 × 379.08 at the existing preview origin instead of stretching the legacy photo to the parent.

The center label always uses the source `MobileBuyInRange` key, native Roboto 12 and #ebf5cd at source six-seat y175. It reads actual validated local min/max rules, falling back to the table’s fixed default stack. Equal bounds show one amount; a range shows min - max. Thousands grouping is retained (9,760); practice amounts do not pretend to be operator dollars. The existing translation machinery handles the title. The CSS 14px linebox and existing parent bounds still require exact native font-metric/raster verification.

No seat, HUD, dealer/blind marker, selection, waiting, admission or wallet handler was replaced. Other game variants retain their existing renderer. Actual profile avatars are preserved: source PreviewPlayerAvatar uses a real URL plus border/badge flags, so the different native capture alone does not justify replacing all local images with silhouettes.

All 18 exact-head checks/reviews passed: Swift exact crop readback, six preview/inheritance/avatar-context and seven font-chain source identities, six font identities, all 130 runtime/1,365 art hashes, cache/privacy/FIFO/HTTP guards, actual fixed120/range60 - 240/9,760 label and image/scale/color/font/position assertions, real Table/Group controls, observer Wait/Cancel, Group funding/replay/two windows, manual Join/preview/HUD, two hands with five computers, independently verified payouts, restart/reconnect and cross-room conservation. A separate isolated after-capture at1200×963 was visually reviewed against the saved original. All PR259 original/before image hashes stayed unchanged. The native client was not launched again.

Guarded idle rollout PID 27857→37426 preserved all six owner files (15,496 bytes), no live gameplay QA. Runtime: 130 files, 1,482,571 bytes. Art: 1,365 files; 1,364 immutable verified hardlinks plus one PNG adding 65,498 bytes. One small runtime snapshot; no dependencies, worktrees, models or large downloads. Previous runtimes/art were preserved. Markdown/report are current; generated HTML/inventories remain historical PR216.

This resolves two confirmed PR259 baseline differences. It is not full preview or app parity: parent bounds/clipping/resampling, exact text metrics, player plates/country/numeric HUD/actual avatar-data branches, promotions, footer/window semantics and native timing remain incomplete or unverified. Continue from the saved reference/source under the background-only constraint; the native launcher’s observed self-activation makes another unattended launch inappropriate.

[Detailed source contract](docs/rea/cash-preview-surface.md).

## Historical release baseline — PR257

PR257 merged `03fa48656abc04850ecd0fa331d2ed43239e4117`; tested source `09f34fe014274c07f6f2490b2744999e7aa93c02`.

Hold’em Table View now uses authenticated server identity to paint every room where the account is seated or queued. Native TableInfo compares waiting UserId with Account.current.id; TableCell refreshes on playing/waiting changes and TableCellRenderer combines the flags. Metrics use original #ff8932, bold and italic with the already installed native Roboto Bold Italic. Table name stays ordinary purple/regular; counter shapes stay unchanged. Selecting another room does not remove the account’s actual status.

The existing cookie-only read-only preview-paint response adds only the current account’s waiting boolean. Actual me identifies a seat; waiting comes from that account in the real room queue. No matching nickname, anonymous seat token or public waiting-player names grant the style. The endpoint still excludes note text, cards, hands, statistics, labels and seat tokens. No new endpoint, asset, font, wallet operation or admission permission was introduced.

The private cache covers actual occupied or queued Table rooms, including a queued room after all seats empty. Same-account unchanged rosters retain paint while other rooms refresh; affected roster/account/view changes abort and invalidate old reads. Account mismatch, malformed boolean and failed reads clear stale status. The selected row’s Wait count uses the confirmed waiting response immediately: 1 after Wait and 0 after Cancel, without waiting for the next public catalog poll. Other updates retain the existing two-second cadence; native notification latency is not established.

All 17 exact-head checks/reviews passed: private HTTP schema/auth/origin/logout/no-write and mucked-card guards; persisted FIFO/offer/expiry/cancel/admission; cache actor/stale/malformed/empty-queue/retained-room cases; source/runtime/art/font identities; real own-row native style and separate observer Wait/Cancel/count/no-seat/no-token/no-debit; Table/Group keyboard/Join, two funded windows/replay, manual Join/preview/HUD, two hands with five computers, independent payouts, restart/reconnect and cross-room conservation. Own and corrected waiting renders were visually inspected. The earlier waiting-count 0 render was kept as regression evidence; final count assertions prove 1/0.

Guarded idle rollout PID 47241→27857 preserved all six owner files (15,496 bytes), with no live gameplay QA. Runtime: 130 files, 1,481,557 bytes. Existing 1,364-art pack reused unchanged. Two small runtime snapshots were generated in this task (1,481,476 and 1,481,557 bytes); no dependencies, worktrees, models or art downloads. Previous runtimes remain preserved. Generated HTML/inventories remain historical PR216; Markdown/report are current.

Full app parity is incomplete. Native physical parent/raster, autoScale/autoSlice, notification/animation/audio timing and operator brand/featured/private/promotion variants remain unverified or unmapped. The existing local queue expiry/admission policy is not a claim about the operator’s private backend.

[Detailed REA contract](docs/rea/cash-table-account.md).

## Historical release baseline — PR253

PR253 merged `9adadd12205797c7157a4865859ee27a779c35f4`; tested source `9616b88c5d5b6113f036135747554a104b8c5c7a`.

Hold’em Table View keeps each actual hosted room separate and applies native-derived descending blind/minimum-buy-in/ante/capacity priorities, then descending actual player count. Equal or unavailable operator indices keep stable input order; no operator numbers are invented. Shared rule ordering preserves Group View behavior. Selection remains attached to the room href across refreshes.

ArrowUp/Down changes the selected row without wrapping. Enter and double click dispatch the local equivalent of native OPEN_TABLE: the actual room opens as a spectator. No join/autojoin intent, seat token, reservation or wallet debit is created. Seating still requires the existing explicit Join/buy-in confirmation. Handlers only apply to the current visible Hold’em list and actual row focus; modal form inputs and other catalogs remain separate.

## Historical observations and implementation checkpoints

The entries below retain earlier findings and their limits. Later verified entries and linked REA topic guides supersede earlier implementation-status statements; the workflow matrix above describes current coverage.

Hosted Hold’em comparison (850 × 615): the owner-created table uses the same felt stylesheet and card renderer as the solo table. Native reference: six-player spectator window; local test: four human sessions. Observed on the Mac: dealer marker, hidden opponent cards, visible board, stacks, posted bets, pot collection and win badge. Local table renders these from room state; collection and reveal are short CSS transitions, not frame-timing matches. Native wait list, chat/emotes, settings, avatar art, and private seated action controls have not been matched. Local invitation, action history, standings, and results panels are functional local features; native panel parity is unverified.

Hosted cash tables now seat six humans or a mix of humans and computer players. In local reference mode the table plays the installed client's action, board, deal, and pot-win MP3s in response to room updates. Sound can be muted and the preference survives reload. A reconnect displays the current hand without replaying old cues. Clip identity is verified locally; live-client event sequencing, overlap, levels and physical speaker output have not been matched. The pot below the board shows collected contributions only, excluding outstanding street bets. Exact timing of collection remains unmeasured.

Earlier read-only notes reported Game Settings → Betting options defaults; a retained native full-panel capture has not been located in the current bounded search. Reported opening defaults 2BB/3BB/4BB/Pot, normal 33%/50%/75%/100%, four shortcuts followed by amount and slider. Local hosted rooms implement those default shortcuts, clamp to legal raise totals, and increment by one big blind when scrolling up. Choosing a size does not submit a bet. Custom preset settings, Enter-to-bet confirmation, and live seated action-button geometry remain unverified or unimplemented.

The hosted table now implements the observed “Always confirm” preference when pressing Enter in the bet amount field. Cancel (or Escape) leaves the hand unchanged; Confirm submits the displayed legal total. The confirmation copy and appearance are local, since the settings reference shows the preference, not the seated prompt.

Showdown presentation: On NLH Diamond 02 the observed fold award gathered outstanding wagers first, began the WIN badge about 0.5 seconds later, and held the center pot until roughly 2 seconds before starting the transfer. Measurements approximate (sampled at 10 fps); local reference excerpt: home-art/native-fold-award.mp4. In local contested hands newly public hole cards turn at settlement; a badge follows beside the winner's stack, then the pot moves and the displayed stack changes on arrival. Refresh displays the settled state without replay, and reduced-motion users get the final state directly. Fold awards do not establish the native timing of a contested card reveal.

Verified owner-hosted returning-player path: create an account, sign in from a fresh browser context, open Hold’em Table View, select the owner-created table, join with the signed-in display name, fill five computer seats, settle the hand and reconnect. The hosted seat credential still lives in sessionStorage; the profile name is convenience, not proof of seat ownership in a new tab. Full player-session recovery after server restart and native screen-by-screen comparisons remain open.

Account-bound hosted seats: the owner-hosted browser test now restarts the game server, uses a fresh browser context, rejects guest recovery by nickname at the full table, signs back in and retakes the original seat with identical stacks and results. Recovery rotates the seat credential. New signed-in seats support this path; legacy/guest seats stay token-only.

Hosted Hold’em preview: Group View lists sitting players, seat numbers and stacks. Table View places the public name and stack at each occupied position. A separate spectator browser kept the same selected table through showdown and displayed all six final balances without refreshing. The API test asserts the entire public seat payload contains only name and stack.

Hosted Hold’em “Open”: an unseated observer now sees the public live hand, board, stacks, action history and payout without advancing play. In a separate browser the test saw 12 card backs and no betting controls while six players were live, then 12 face-up cards after a six-way showdown. Folded hands stay excluded by the shared visibility rule. Spectating a table does not register a player or add them to a wait list.

Cash lobby Table View now populates the observed AvgPot and VPIP% columns from the last 20 local completed hands. Average pot excludes uncalled returns; VPIP counts each dealt seat at most once per hand when it calls or raises preflop. Older hands without preflop tracking are omitted from VPIP. Six isolated players produced a 372-chip pot and 83% VPIP (five of six); a spectator browser also verified that AvgPot updates after showdown. The native aggregation window is unknown.

Switch Chip Display: the native settings page identifies Big Blind as the selected alternative and says clicking a stack switches display. Hosted local tables now offer an opt-in stack click, an open-in-BB preference and chip/BB formatting of the felt. Six-seat browser test: 120 chips converted to 30 BB and the 6-chip pot to 1.5 BB; clicking the stack restored chips and preserved the selected 12-chip raise. Bet entry remains in play chips. Native cash currency conversions are not implemented.

Four-card Omaha: Home → PLO lists local owner-created six-seat tables. The browser acceptance test (node network-browser-test.mjs --plo) signs in, seats a player with five computers, checks spectator privacy, independently calculates the showdown payout, and recovers the same seat after a server restart. The engine enforces pot-limit raises and exactly two hole cards plus three board cards. A live Omaha Practice table is available with 200-chip stacks and 2/4 blinds. The saved PLO capture also shows five- and six-card game filters; those variants and native visual parity are still open.

All / PLO / PLO-5 / PLO-6 filter the local Omaha catalog. Admins can host five- and six-card Omaha. Separate browser runs for both variants verified a complete six-seat hand, hidden opposing cards until showdown, an independently calculated payout, and authenticated recovery after restart. The variant-specific seated layout and native sound are not verified.

Six-card showdown, 850 × 615 browser viewport: a side seat previously extended 41.9 px beyond the felt. Omaha cards now overlap within each hand. The browser test measures all six face-up hands after the payout animation: all 36 card positions remain within the horizontal felt boundaries and successive cards expose at least 48% of the preceding face width. This verifies local geometry, not native Omaha seat spacing.

Hosted Omaha lobby return: after showdown a signed-in player can return to the PLO catalog and reopen the same table without rejoining. Browser verification checks the displayed profile, catalog mode and all six settled stacks before exercising server restart recovery separately.

Hosted Hold’em continuous play: the returning player now exercises Ready for next hand after restart in the browser acceptance test. All six closing balances from hand one carry forward, the button moves to seat 2, seats 3 and 4 post blinds, and the second completed hand leaves two results and the original 720 play chips.

Audio evidence: ffprobe reports a single video stream and no audio stream in native-fold-award.mp4 (3.244 seconds). It cannot establish original sound timing. Batched local updates now play AllIn only for a player's final chip payment; a regression test covers a call followed by an all-in across a spectator poll.

Native client was on Game Settings with no spectator table open during the audio check. Verified all nine live localhost cue responses byte-for-byte against their installed Sound*.mp3 files; source digests and clip durations are in home-art/rea/local-sound-comparison.json. This proves asset identity, not native playback timing or selection.

Owner-created freezeout: four separate browser contexts created local accounts, entered the Tournaments catalog, selected the owner's event and registered with their signed-in names. Seven hands produced three eliminations, a heads-up finish, and one winner with 400 tournament chips and the 100-credit practice prize. Restart preserved the prize. The registration link now carries player identity; closed hosted events have no registration link.

Tournament catalog now uses the observed Start / Event Name / Buy-in / Players / Prize columns and queries local registration state. The 1200 × 720 comparison in home-art/tournaments-side-by-side.png shows the remaining differences in navigation, filters and details. Browser verification registered four signed-in players from the revised event list and confirmed the winner's 100-credit payout survived restart.

Tournament top selection now uses the observed selected Tournaments and inactive Poker artwork. The live 1200 × 720 comparison has been refreshed. Tournament-specific category navigation remains unimplemented.

Tournament search verification: separate signed-in players searched for mixed-case pLaYeR1 after the first registration. Only that player's hosted event remained; clearing the search restored both events. The resulting four-player tournament completed and retained its winner's prize after restart.

Tournament selected-event panel now separates start condition, buy-in, player count, prize, starting chips, blinds, format and payout. Browser assertions checked the actual count and pool for each of four successive registrations (0–3 players, 0–75 credits) before the four-player event completed and its 100-credit prize survived restart.

Tournament panel comparison refreshed at 1200 × 720: search occupies the top of the right panel, labeled event details follow it, and Register anchors to the bottom as observed in the native capture. Remaining visible differences include tournament category navigation and native filter controls.

Home control audit: home-art/rea/home-control-gap-audit.json enumerates every button/tab from the captured Home layout, current enabled state, and available native observation. This is not a complete nested-screen inventory. Search selection/clear and tournament sort-selection preservation have browser regression coverage.

Short Deck evaluation: all 1,404 rank classes in the installed hand_rank_shortdeck.txt compare in the same strict order in the independent evaluator. Seven-card selection tests cover flush over trips and the A6789 straight; duplicate and out-of-deck cards are rejected. The mode is not yet connected to hosted dealing or its ante/button-blind betting structure.

Short Deck dealing primitive: shared shuffle supports a validated 36-card six-through-ace deck. Thirty generated decks passed uniqueness and rank checks; standard 52-card no-limit regression passed. Official GGPoker introduction describes everyone posting antes and a separate button ante, but its text says sizing varies; native exact live-blind/ante allocation is not yet verified. Do not substitute standard small/big blinds.

Short Deck engine prototype now posts one dead ante per seat and one equal live button blind, deals from 36 cards, acts left of the button and settles using the shipped rank order. Six equal stacks produced a checked-through 48-chip pot and conserved 720 chips. Native lobby capture displays equal paired stakes (for example $10 ($10)), consistent with this configuration, but live forced-bet allocation has not been observed; hosted activation remains pending that verification.

Short Deck edge cases: 24 engine hands cover every button seat with four unequal-stack configurations, including stacks smaller than the ante. Tests assert bounded completion, legal call costs, no negative balances, unique dealt cards, payout/contribution equality and conservation after every action. They do not establish native forced-bet allocation or independently verify each side-pot winner.

Exact Short Deck side-pot fixture: six players contribute 20/40/80/120/120/120. On Qh 8h 6h Ac Ad, Ah Kh wins the 120-chip main pot; 9h Th wins 260 across the next side pots, beating aces full under Short Deck rules; 6c 6d wins the remaining 120. The engine payout vector matches [120,0,260,0,120,0], preserving all 500 chips.

REA native observation attempt: capture-native-ui-scenario against running native GGPoker PID 57441/window 3152 returned capability_unavailable: Selected application does not expose accessibility windows. Result retained in home-art/rea/native-ui-accessibility-result.json. AX navigation cannot currently establish Short Deck forced-bet behavior; window screenshots remain available.

Visual desktop control attempt: CUA inventory identifies the running original as com.ggnetwork.desktop.launcher.live.ggpcom, distinct from the inactive com.nsuslab launcher entry and local GGPoker PlayMoney. Selecting the running original app timed out (computer-use server timeoutReached). CoreGraphics independently confirms native PLAYGG.RO PID 57441/window 3152 remains on screen. Do not restart the original client or infer that it stopped from this control timeout.

Tournament lobby current blinds: hosted summary exposes only currentBlind and level in addition to its existing public summary; selected-event details use the current blind instead of hardcoded opening 2/4. Browser tournament test checks summary blind/level against actual game state after each increase. Server restart is needed to load this summary change in the long-running live instance.

Live update: queried every hosted room and confirmed no active hand before restarting the local server. Compared /api/hosted before/after with deep equality: all four table definitions, six occupied Hold’em seats, hand numbers, public stacks and statistics survived. Current tournament-summary implementation is now loaded in the live process.

Local owner audit: successful table create/update/close appends timestamped before/after settings to owner-audit.jsonl with 0600 permissions. Owner tests verify persistence through restart, close history, no owner credential in records, no audit file after a rejected initial write, and HTTP denial. Table save and audit append are separate filesystem writes; the admin form explicitly reports an audit failure after a successful table save. This independent local admin feature does not claim access to native private admin behavior.

Owner audit failure injection passed: making the audit destination unwritable as a file yielded saved:true/auditSaved:false while preserving the table save. Restoring the audit path resumed logging without server restart. Live process updated after confirming no active hands; hosted table snapshots before/after compare equal.

Owner table list Edit loads existing ID, name, stack, blind, format and admission state into the console without touching its owner key. Real browser test loads the selected table, closes it (join link removed), reopens it, seats a signed-in player with five computers, plays two hands and verifies restart recovery and chip conservation. Available immediately as a client-side change.

Six-card Omaha admin edit regression: browser loaded PLO-6 settings through Edit, closed and reopened admission, completed showdown with an independently verified 120-chip award, returned through PLO lobby, recovered after restart and played a second hand. Inspected home-art/plo6-occupied-showdown.png: all six hands and all 36 face-up hole cards remain visible at 850 × 615. This is a local inspection; no matching occupied native PLO-6 capture exists yet.

Home settings now opens a live local menu for Sound, Hot Key and Switch Chip Display, reusing hosted-table preferences. Browser test changes all three in Home, verifies the hosted table opens muted with hotkeys enabled and BB stacks, then switches back to chips and sound, plays two hands and recovers after restart. This is a partial settings menu, not full native menu parity. Home audit now counts settings as enabled (9 enabled, 36 disabled).

Home settings presentation: right-aligned menu under the settings icon, with native-style gray/green switches. Escape closes the menu and restores focus to Settings; outside clicks dismiss it. Browser regression verifies Escape/focus plus preferences carried into two hosted hands and restart recovery. Exact full-menu geometry remains unverified because most native settings entries are still absent.

Owner audit viewer added to the console, showing the latest 100 timestamped create/update records with table names and admission states. API tests verify owner-key enforcement, foreign-origin rejection and equality to the persisted log. A 1 MiB read limit bounds viewer memory; larger logs remain on disk for direct inspection. Live backend restart and browser verification of the viewer remain pending.

Owner audit viewer browser verification passed: create, close and reopen produced three displayed records, including exactly one Closed record. The same session then hosted two hands and recovered after restart. Live backend updated with no active hand; before/after hosted snapshots match and authenticated audit request succeeds while guest access returns 401.

Official Short Deck rule cross-check: https://ke-contents.ggpoker10.com/poker-games/short-deck/ explicitly limits tables to six seats. Engine now rejects seven or more Short Deck seats while preserving existing Hold’em capacities; regression passed. Official paired play-money stakes are consistent with equal ante/blind notation, but do not resolve exact live/dead allocation.

Tournament keyboard focus: rebuilding event rows after selection or public data changes preserves focus by stable room ID. Four-browser test explicitly focuses and selects the owner's event, asserts focus remains on that row, then exercises registration, full tournament payout and restart.

Integrated regression checkpoint: all 11 standalone test suites passed on the current source. A separate six-browser owner-hosted acceptance run verified private cards, flop reveal, an all-in, folded contributions and a 372-chip payout. Observed local animation events included showCard, collect, turnOver, markWinner, releasePot and payout. This establishes current local regression health, not native timing or full feature parity.

Short Deck hosted backend added with explicitly labeled local ante/button-blind rules. Owner API test created a six-seat table, checked its two private six-through-ace cards and 12/6/6/6/6/6 forced contributions, restarted, recovered and completed a 72-chip hand across six authenticated seat sessions with 1440 chips retained. Browser catalog routing and full-hand visual acceptance remain pending; native forced-bet allocation is still unverified.

Home → Short Deck now routes to locally hosted tables with paired ante/button-blind notation and an explicit local-rules heading. Browser acceptance (--shortdeck) edits and opens an owner table, signs in, joins with five computers, validates a showdown payout independently using short-deck ranking, returns via the correct lobby, recovers after restart and plays hand two with 720 chips retained. Native forced-bet allocation and visual parity remain unverified; live server activation is pending.

Live Short Deck Practice available: six seats, 160 starting chips, 4-chip ante and equal button blind under explicit local rules. Update preserved existing room snapshots. Captured local and native lobby at 1200 × 838 in home-art/shortdeck-side-by-side.png; comparison exposed incorrect promotional header, corrected using the Short Deck capture. Column count/layout still differs from native five-column list.

Short Deck Group View now matches the native five-column structure: Promotion, Stakes, Buy-in, Players, Action. Join cell remains a real navigation target after removing the extra column. Browser acceptance verifies five header/row cells plus two hands, payouts, lobby return and restart recovery. Refreshed/inspected 1200 × 838 comparison: native left, live local right, in home-art/shortdeck-side-by-side.png.

Short Deck folded-winner fixture: folding Ah Kh excludes the strongest flush from settlement while retaining its 8 forced chips. Independent expected payouts [0,0,368,0,120,0] match actual; folded player's 12 uncommitted chips remain and total wealth stays 500. Home control audit updated to include Short Deck as enabled; enabled does not imply native parity.

Short Deck nextDealer regression: heads-up local hands use a button blind, so Hold’em's two-seat big-blind rotation could leave the same dealer in place. The shared helper now advances Short Deck directly to the next funded seat. Two-seat and sparse-seat assertions failed before the fix and pass after it; Hold’em dealer tests still pass. Current six-seat cash room rotation is unchanged.

All-In or Fold engine prototype: native AoF capture shows four seats and a Hold’em 10/20 row with 160 buy-in. Shared engine aof variant requires four seats and permits only allin/fold; checks, partial calls and arbitrary raises are rejected. Tests cover a folded seat excluded from a 96-chip showdown and uncontested fold awards with chip conservation. Lobby, hosted UI, rebuy rules and jackpots are not yet implemented.

AoF hosted rooms: four-seat capacity, three computer opponents, mode-specific allin/fold actions, timeout fold and persisted recovery are implemented. Direct room integration test completes an all-in showdown and preserves all 128 chips across storage reload. Hosted table UI renders only Fold and All in for an active AoF turn. Browser journey, Home routing, native buy-in/rebuy and jackpots remain open.

AoF browser acceptance now passes through owner creation, seating with three computers, exact Fold/All in control set, finished showdown, 128-chip balance total after animation, and refresh recovery. Dedicated --aof branch reuses the existing browser/server harness. Home routing, live activation and native payout/rebuy/jackpot behavior remain incomplete.

Home AoF routing browser acceptance passed: native Home AoF button opens locally hosted AoF tables, selection joins the tested four-seat table, and back navigation returns to AoF before reopening the same settled hand. Controls, 128-chip total and refresh recovery remain passing. Live backend activation and native lobby-specific columns still pending.

AoF live practice table activated with four seats, 32 starting chips and 2/4 blinds. Existing hosted snapshots survived server update. Same-size native/local comparison captured at 1200 × 720 (home-art/aof-side-by-side.png) exposed incorrect shared banner and panel width; corrected with native AoF banner and 69%/31% catalog split. Native game filters, table-view columns, rebuys and jackpots still differ.

AoF All / Hold’em / Omaha filters now operate on real hosted table modes. Browser verification selects Omaha (no implemented tables, no join target), then Hold’em (one matching playable table), and completes the hand, refresh and lobby-return checks. AoF Omaha remains unimplemented; the empty filter does not imply game support.

AoF Table View now uses nine observed headings including Games and All-in %, rather than the cash lobby's VPIP column. Hold’em identifies the implemented variant, average pot remains local real data, and unavailable All-in %/hands-per-hour show em dashes. Browser assertions verify headers and variant/stat cells before playing and recovering a four-seat hand.

AoF selected-table preview uses shipped Preview_Aof_Tourney.png as a clean felt/rail layer with four live seat labels over it. It contains no baked player names or controls. Browser checks verify four seat nodes and loaded artwork before playing the hand. The source is the client's tournament preview asset; exact cash-preview artwork/geometry remains unverified.

AoF browser payout proof strengthened: all four seats contribute 32; separate best-hand evaluation over revealed cards derives winners and odd-chip distribution and compares the entire payout vector. Test then restarts the server and checks identical payouts/stacks through the same browser seat. Latest run independently awarded [0,0,0,128].

AoF All-in % now counts seats that moved their remaining chips in during each of the last 20 locally completed hands, divided by dealt seats. A four-player all-in yields 100%, verified via room reload and full browser test; no hands yields no statistic. Native aggregation/window semantics remain unknown. Live backend update pending.

AoF statistics migration: completed hands missing all-in tracking are excluded from both numerator and denominator. Storage test removes tracked data from an old result and verifies summary returns null instead of 0%. Live server updated after checking for active hands; existing table/balance snapshots preserved, new AoF history correctly unknown.

AoF Fold browser path (--aof --fold-player): player folds before committing chips and retains 32; three computers contribute 32 each, payout independently evaluates to [0,0,96,0], total balance remains 128. Folded player receives no payout, local All-in % is 75%, and exact balances/payouts survive refresh, lobby return and server restart.

Home settings now includes the observed Open table in big blinds preference, disabled unless Switch Chip Display is enabled. It shares river-open-bb with hosted tables. Browser test toggles the preference off/on, verifies persisted values, enters a BB-formatted table and completes two hands with restart recovery.

Six-seat selected-table preview now shows the same seat-indexed avatar artwork as its playable hosted table, only for occupied seats. Names and balances remain public live room data. Browser spectator test verifies six avatar elements, player-specific accessible labels and six stacks, then confirms balances update after showdown. Exact native preview avatar geometry remains unverified.

Occupied six-seat lobby preview inspected at 1200 × 931 (home-art/holdem-occupied-preview.png). All six live seat labels, avatars and balances are readable in the selected-table panel. The table preview still visibly differs from the native felt/rail artwork; avatar readability does not establish pixel parity. The capturing browser run also passed two hands and restart recovery.

Standard six-seat preview artwork identified by visual inspection in shipped assets/preview/tpt_0.png, copied as clean standard-table-preview.png. Hold’em/PLO/Short Deck preview now uses that felt/rail layer with dynamic occupied seats. Captured/inspected updated 1200 × 931 screenshot: six names, stacks and avatars remain readable on the green table. Browser two-hand and restart acceptance passed; exact native geometry is not asserted.

Hotkey editing guard now uses the browser's isContentEditable state, covering empty and inherited contenteditable attributes in addition to standard form inputs. Browser regression focuses an empty-attribute editable region and sends F1 on a live turn; the hand version stays unchanged. Two-hand/restart acceptance still passes.

Local sign-out added to signed-in Home settings. Same-origin POST invalidates the local account session and expires its cookie; current tab removes its hosted seat tokens before returning to login. Browser verifies /local/me returns 401, current-tab token removal, sign-in again, two hosted hands and account-bound restart recovery. Other tabs' independent seat tokens are not revoked; native logout parity and live backend activation remain pending.

Local logout security regression: foreign-origin POST and GET cannot invalidate a valid session; same-origin POST expires the cookie and rejects reuse of the old token, while the account can sign in again. Live backend updated after all hands settled; hosted snapshots unchanged. The route is idempotent for a guest.

Hosted join now accepts an optional exact seat. Join form offers Any open seat or numbered positions, marks occupied positions unavailable, and server rejects fractional/out-of-range/occupied selections. Room integration verifies selection of seat 3 and rejection paths. Default auto-seat browser AoF acceptance still passes; explicit selection browser journey and live backend activation are pending.

Open seat markers are now accessible Take seat N buttons that submit the chosen seat through normal join validation. Browser --aof --seat3 clicks seat 3 on the felt, fills three computers, independently verifies the payout, and confirms seat index 2 and exact balances after server restart. Dropdown path also passed previously. Live backend update remains pending.

Seat selection live activation: all hosted hands were settled before server update; full /api/hosted snapshot (tables, occupants, stacks, statistics) matches after restart. Live network script matches the tested source. Explicit seat3 browser acceptance previously verified selection, payout and same-seat restart recovery.

Seat validation moved before room creation: malformed seat requests no longer create/persist empty rooms. Regression checks the room store after a rejected fractional seat. HTTP owner integration also submits a competing join to an occupied seat and verifies 409 with occupancy unchanged at one, then fills the remaining six-seat table normally.

Local table chat added for seated players, with 280-character validation, latest-50 retention and text-only DOM rendering. Room integration verifies sender identity, literal markup preservation, unchanged hand state, spectator visibility and storage recovery. Before-hand join/fill controls use the normal full room response. Native chat layout, browser acceptance and live activation remain pending.

Table chat browser acceptance: one isolated player sent literal markup, another displayed it as text with no image elements; turn and committed chips were unchanged. Six-browser all-in/fold/payout acceptance still passed. Live backend updated with all hands settled; before/after hosted snapshots match.

Chat per-sender limit fixed: a second sender no longer resets the first sender's one-second send interval. Integration verifies alternating sender rejection, unauthorized sender rejection and 281-character rejection. Message list now has bounded scrolling and long-word wrapping. Backend limit change awaits next live update; text-layout change serves immediately.

Native runtime recheck: original PLAYGG.RO remains on Switch Chip Display settings at window 3152 (1200 × 961). System Events can make GGnet frontmost, but foreground-targeted clicks on the observed Sound row produced no visual change in two bounded attempts. Stopped repeating that unchanged route. Native audio/forced-bet observation remains unavailable through current control paths; local implementation can continue independently.

Post-seat/chat PLO-6 regression: full owner-hosted browser path passed on current source, including local sign-out/login, settings propagation, six private hole cards, 36-card spectator reveal, clipped-hand bounds, independently evaluated 120-chip payout, PLO lobby return, account-bound restart recovery, second-hand dealer movement and 720-chip conservation.

Three shipped static emoticons visually inspected (angry, listening, laughing) and connected to validated local table-chat reactions. Server accepts only reaction IDs 1–3 and shares/persists them under the same sender limits. Integration verifies laughing reaction identity and rejects unknown ID. These are local chat reactions using native artwork; native reaction menu layout, animation, browser verification and live backend activation remain pending.

Reaction browser acceptance passed: a second isolated player sends Laughing and the first receives the shipped 200px artwork; wagers remain unchanged. Six-player hand independently split 372 chips as 186/186 with folded contributions included. Live backend updated with all hands settled; hosted snapshot preserved and all three reaction images respond.

Table reactions now show shipped artwork beside the sender's occupied seat for four seconds, while chat retains the record. Picker buttons show the artwork with accessible labels. Six-browser test verifies Player 2's Laughing image arrives at the corresponding seat in Player 1's view without changing wagers, then completes the hand/payout checks. Four-second duration and placement are local behavior, not verified native timing.

Account registration persistence fix: in-memory account map is committed only after temp-file write and atomic rename succeed. Failure injection blocks the temp path, verifies registration throws without permitting login to a phantom account, then restores storage and successfully retries the same nickname. Local profile and owner suites pass; backend activation pending.

Shipped-rule investigation found Game/51/IronDesktop.swf (41.8 MB uncompressed FWS). REA analyze rejects SWF as unsupported binary format; provider result retained. Bounded direct symbol inventory finds ButtonAnte, ButtonBlind, PostedButtonBlind and ShortDeckGameRules (65 matching symbols), with source digest in home-art/rea/shortdeck-shipped-symbols.json. Symbol presence does not prove the forced-bet algorithm.

SWF tool availability audit: no ffdec/JPEXS, RABCDAsm or swfdump found in existing local tool/application directories; Java launcher reports no installed runtime. Bounded printable-string scan of IronDesktop.swf found no explanatory ante/button-blind rule text (six incidental matches). Do not infer native algorithm from symbols or install a large toolchain merely to repeat string inspection. Continue supported local feature work while native evidence remains open.

Reaction trust-boundary validation now requires an integer allowlisted ID. Tests reject numeric strings, null, arrays, objects (including non-coercible objects) and fractions, verify the room version remains unchanged, and preserve normal reactions. This prevents malformed reaction objects from surfacing as server errors. Backend change queued with registration persistence fix for next safe live update.

Bet-entry continuity: unrelated room/chat updates preserve a legal draft bet and input focus when hand number, action history length and acting seat remain unchanged. A changed betting state resets through normal legal options. Browser prepares a nondefault amount, sends chat and verifies the amount survives, then completes two hands and restart recovery. Served live as client-only change.

Chat-only updates now refresh chat and seat reactions without rebuilding betting controls when the rest of public state is identical. Browser opens Enter-to-bet confirmation, receives a reaction and verifies the identical dialog stays open; no bet is submitted. Prepared bet and two-hand/restart regression still pass.

Reaction updates no longer rebuild the felt: only reaction images update, preserving active payout/collection elements and delayed stack changes. Browser captures a payout element, sends a reaction during settlement and verifies the same element remains while the message arrives; animation ordering, final balances, second hand and restart pass. Reactions are also matched to current seat occupant names to avoid displaying a departed player's reaction on a replacement.

Integrated regression checkpoint after chat, reactions, chosen-seat join and profile changes: all 14 standalone suites pass. Native-observation blockers still affect forced-bet, seated-control and timing parity; missing Rush/Spin/Battle Royale and multi-table tournament workflows remain open.

Spin investigation: native observed-spin2.png shows both 3-Max and 6-Max, selectable buy-in tiles, number-of-games controls, Play Now and insurance. Official https://ggpoker.com/poker-games/spin-gold/ confirms multiplier-dependent starting chips/blinds/time bank and simultaneous-elimination ordering by starting stack, then clockwise from button. Its rendered HTML omits prize/probability rows; no distribution has been inferred. Spin remains unimplemented.

Tournament finishing positions now use each hand's initial stacks. Simultaneous eliminations rank larger initial stacks higher, then clockwise from the button, following the official Spin & Gold rule. Standings display known finishing places. Unit tests cover unequal/equal stacks and previous eliminations; four-browser tournament verifies unique places 1–4 and place 1 matches the winner, with existing payout/restart checks passing. Legacy hands without initialStacks are not assigned guessed tied places.

Spin prize-data investigation: official global page rejects direct source retrieval with HTTP 403; no header/identity workaround attempted. Official Canada readable page also has empty prize-table bodies. Installed Spin JSON files are wheel motion/table artwork metadata, not prize-frequency configuration. Probability/stack/blind tables remain missing; random weights must not be invented or borrowed from a different jurisdiction.

Hosted tournament backend accepts explicit 3/4/6 seats (default remains four). Starting stacks, elimination slots and loops now follow capacity. Integration plays complete three- and six-player events, verifies all finishing places, chip conservation, 75/150-credit prize pools and storage recovery. This is configurable freezeout infrastructure, not an implementation of Spin's random prizes. Admin form, player copy/layout and browser acceptance for new capacities remain pending.

Admin tournament capacity selector and dynamic player copy now connected. Configuration uses tournamentSeats to avoid collision with public seats[] previews; browser edit/close/reopen caught and fixed that collision. Existing four-player browser event passes; three/six-player engine integration remains passing. Explicit browser events for new capacities and live activation remain pending.

Three-player browser tournament now passes: admin selects three seats, three isolated local accounts register, five hands progress through heads-up, scheduled blinds increase, unique places 1–3 are assigned, winner finishes with 300 chips and 75 practice credits, and the prize survives server restart. Browser harness supports --event-seats=3/4/6; six-player run still pending.

Six-player browser event passed: six isolated accounts registered through the revised admin/catalog UI; seven hands yielded five eliminations, heads-up, unique places 1–6, 600 chips to winner and a 150-credit prize preserved after restart. Backend batch (capacity, places, registration persistence and reaction validation) activated live after all hands settled, with hosted snapshots unchanged.

Hosted freezeouts can now fill empty seats with computers before the first deal. Simulated entries correctly fund the full prize pool, and normal tournament scheduling/elimination applies. New integration test completes three- and six-player events with one human and two/five bots, conserves 300/600 chips, awards 75/150 practice credits, and preserves final tournament data after reload. Browser verification and live activation pending.

Solo three-player browser event passed: admin selected three seats, one human joined and filled two computers, nine hands completed to a winner, total 300 chips and 75-credit prize were verified, and tournament results survived actual server restart. --solo-event --event-seats=3/6 is now available in the existing browser harness. Live activation of tournament bot filling remains pending.

Solo six-seat browser event passed with five computers, actual UI decisions, winner, 600 conserved chips, 150-credit prize and server restart recovery. Tournament bot filling activated live after all hosted hands settled. Added empty three- and six-player practice events to the local Tournaments catalog; existing tables/balances preserved.

Owner Duplicate action creates a new table draft with unique ID, copied format/limits/capacity and fresh open admission; original table/results are untouched. Browser verifies no table is created until Save, copied settings match, Save adds a distinct table, and original table still completes two hands with restart recovery. This is an independent local admin convenience, not observed native private admin behavior.

Host ownership fixed for chosen seats: first join to seat 3 remains the host after another human joins seat 1. Host is persisted explicitly; nonhost Fill computers is rejected and reload preserves control. Before-hand host departure transfers control to a remaining human. Legacy rooms retain their prior inferred host rather than giving control to a new join.

Rush evidence: native observed-rush.png shows Hold’em/PLO pool filters and pool populations; official https://ggpoker.com/poker-games/rush-cash/ establishes immediate new-table/new-opponent hand after fold. Engine foundation adds validated out-of-turn quickFold for rush hands, retaining committed chips and settling the old hand correctly. Tests cover unchanged acting seat/contributions, folded payout exclusion and last-player awards. Matchmaking, released-stack accounting, lobby, Cash Drops and full Rush play remain unimplemented.

Rush prototype boundary: quickFold currently rejects a seat already matching the positive street price (including an unopened big blind). This prevents an invalid uncontested pot when every contributing seat abandons the hand before a noncontributor acts. Native exact availability still needs observation; this prototype is not exposed as playable Rush.

Rush pool prototype now transfers only uncommitted chips to a new hand with five different waiting opponents while the old table continues. Detached old stacks are excluded from pool wealth; eventual settlements credit exactly once without overwriting a newer assignment. Dealer rotates across new tables. Twelve-player test verifies both hands settle independently with 1200 chips throughout. Queue fairness, persistence, network/browser integration and native Cash Drops remain incomplete.

Rush recovery checkpoint: pool snapshot restores simultaneous old/new hands and settled balances. A fresh Node process restores an in-flight snapshot, completes both tables and verifies all 1200 chips and waiting assignments. Corrupt chip totals, missing active-seat assignments, invalid detached seats and nonboolean settlement flags are rejected. Targeted Rush pool, quick-fold and no-limit suites plus syntax check pass. This is engine persistence infrastructure; no Rush network/lobby/browser path or native Cash Drops is claimed.

Rush server foundation: local-profile-only /api/rush endpoints support join, deal, action, quick-fold and private state. A global version rejects stale actions; POST requires the exact local origin and bounded JSON. Pool roster/active tables persist atomically in rush.json; a failed write restores the prior in-memory checkpoint. Views omit the deck and unrevealed opponent cards, and expose the player's latest settled result. Twelve-account HTTP acceptance verifies immediate five-new-opponent rematch while the old hand continues, real server restart plus login/recovery, both payouts and 2400 conserved chips. Service tests additionally inject write failure. Existing owner-hosted acceptance passes. Rush lobby/table UI, automatic queue matching, turn deadlines, computer pool, native Cash Drops and visual parity remain incomplete; live server has not been restarted for this API-only change.

Rush playable UI: Home Rush tab and local Hold’em pool now reach the shared six-seat renderer. Join/find-table, legal betting, out-of-turn quick fold, new-opponent transfer and latest settled hand are connected to real pool state. Hand-scoped action versions prevent unrelated table actions from rejecting wagers while replayed stale actions remain rejected. Twelve isolated browser contexts play both overlapping hands through buttons, verify private two-card views, new opponents, displayed settlement, lobby return and 2400 total chips. Regular one-human/five-computer Hold’em regression passes two hands, animations and reconnect. 1200x720 captures saved as rush-local-lobby.png and rush-local-table.png; compared with observed-rush.png, promotional banner, leaderboard, multiple pools and native active-tab styling remain different. No PLO Rush, Cash Drops, solo bots, automatic matching or timeout management claimed. Live backend activated after all hands settled; all eight hosted snapshots compare exactly equal across restart and unauthenticated Rush is rejected.

Solo Rush: registered local players can add eleven explicitly named computers and deal a six-seat hand; quick-fold reaches five different waiting computers while the old five finish independently. Computer decisions reuse computerMove, advance at most one action per table per state request with a 500 ms minimum, persist with table checkpoints, and roll back on save errors. Quick-fold binds to table identity and rechecks current legality; bets retain strict hand-history versions. Solo browser acceptance passes actual restart/login between overlapping hands, both completed hands, independently recalculated payouts and 2400 chips. Twelve-human HTTP and service regressions pass. Activated live after all hands settled; eight hosted snapshots preserved. Computers advance only while state is polled; automatic matching, human timeouts, PLO Rush, Cash Drops and native timing/visual parity remain open.

Rush scheduling and waiting queue: server ticks every 500 ms independently of browser polling. Persisted per-turn deadlines allow computer actions and a local 90-second human timeout (check if free, fold if facing chips); timed-out identities pause from future matching. Find-next-table and a quick fold without enough opponents persist a waiting request and match when five eligible opponents become available. Cancel waiting queue persists a pause and excludes that identity from another player's match. Clock-injection tests verify expiry boundaries, restart-preserved deadlines, automatic recovered matching and cancellation. Browser leaves the Rush page for about:blank and observes server-side computer history progress without requests; two hands, independent payouts, actual restart/login and 2400 chips still pass. Live update applied with no active hands and eight existing hosted snapshots unchanged. Timeout duration is local policy, not observed native parity; multi-pool/PLO Rush, Cash Drops, fairness and native animations/sound/layout remain incomplete.

Rush visual pass: 1200x720 native observed-rush.png supplies the exact static banner, selected tab and ribbon regions documented with source digest/bounds in home-art/rea/rush-graphic-regions.json. Live pool rows and entry buttons remain state-driven. Header/filter geometry is adjusted against the capture; unavailable PLO filter now removes the entry link instead of routing to Hold’em, and the unrelated three-computer practice shortcut is hidden in Rush. Twelve-browser functional acceptance and filter/entry checks pass; refreshed rush-local-lobby.png remains visibly incomplete in the leaderboard, pool count and account area. Native recording audit hashes three previously unindexed videos, confirms absent audio and records five-second sampled fold awards/in-progress hands without claiming contested reveal coverage.

PLO Rush connected end to end: independent pool/checkpoint file and authenticated /api/rush/plo endpoints alongside backward-compatible Hold’em routes. PLO pool deals four cards, preserves pot-limit raise caps and exactly-two-hole-card evaluation while allowing validated quick-fold transfer. Hold’em/PLO filters now display and enter their actual pools with separate population counts. Service and twelve-account HTTP tests verify private cards, pool isolation, new opponents, restart/login and 2400 chips. Solo PLO browser starts through the Rush PLO lobby filter, fills eleven computers, proves server-only progression, quick-folds, restarts, completes both hands and independently evaluates payouts using exactly two hole and three board cards. Existing PLO, Rush, queue/deadline and Hold’em HTTP/browser regressions pass. Live activation preserved all hosted/saved Rush snapshots. Captured rush-plo-local-table.png verifies four-card presentation locally, not native occupied-table parity.

Full Switch Chip Display screen: gear menu now reaches a native-structured Game Settings page with the observed twelve-category sidebar, full chip/BB panel, disabled currency choices, preview artwork and OK/Cancel. Four existing gear preferences remain live; only Switch Chip Display is enabled in the full sidebar. Changes in the full panel remain drafts until OK; Cancel/Escape discard and restore focus. Browser acceptance verifies state, disabled dependency, preview, persistence/reload and the main options box within four pixels of native 1200x961 geometry. Same-size native/local comparison added to home-review.html. Regular five-computer Hold’em passes two hands, independent payouts and recovery after the settings changes. Native control reattempt through the running app's correct bundle ID returns AppleEvent -1712 timeout; no restart or account action performed. Native Currency/other settings behavior and full settings parity remain open.

Shared preferences now synchronize across already-open lobby/table tabs in the same browser profile. Storage events refresh chips/BB, hotkey enablement and audio mute without rebuilding the betting panel or submitting an action. Changes at the table also update Home preferences. A full-panel draft remains untouched by external changes until OK/Cancel; clean panels follow external settings. Browser test shares a real storage context, prepares a legal bet, changes all preferences in both directions, verifies the identical input/value and unchanged action history, and checks draft conflict/cancellation. Audio cue tests and syntax checks pass; physical speaker output/native sound timing are unverified. Renamed the misclassified native-sizing-flop.png to local-sizing-flop.png after visual inspection; audit digest/classification retained in home-art/rea/sizing-capture-audit.json.

Scheduled freezeouts: admin accepts an optional local date/time; occupied-event schedule edits are locked and invalid/new past times are rejected. Start date appears in tournament rows/details and the waiting table. Server timer starts with at least two registrations at the due time, funds only occupied seats and closes late registration. Zero/one-player events cancel with recorded simulated entry refunds; no real wallet is implied. Hosted registrations now survive idle periods beyond four hours. Unit acceptance covers exact time boundary, sparse two-of-six seating, complete 200-chip/50-credit event, restart, cancellation/refunds, empty cancellation and long registration retention. HTTP acceptance proves actual timer start after server restart without requests, admin validation/lock, full event and persisted prize. Browser admin schedules a three-player event, registers/fills two computers without early dealing, plays eleven hands to a winner, verifies 300 chips/75 credits and restarts. Existing capacity/bot/owner suites pass. Live update preserved every hosted and saved Rush snapshot. Minimum-two/cancellation policy is independent local admin behavior; native scheduled-event rules/geometry are not claimed.

Tournament navigation/filter pass: native-captured format bar overlays the cash-mode tabs, with only the local Tournaments format enabled. All/PLO category filtering now acts on real event formats; the PLO category was initially empty and now lists implemented PLO freezeouts (see latest entry). Buy-in All/Custom, inclusive min/max range, invalid-range lockout and Reset act on the actual fixed 25-credit events. Preset Low/Medium/High/Super thresholds and branded categories remain disabled rather than guessed. Tournament Lobby opens the selected local event in-app; no-result views clear both navigation and registration links. Browser acceptance covers range/exclusion/reset/category actions and lobby navigation. Four independent browser accounts complete seven hands, levels, eliminations/heads-up, 400 chips and a 100-credit prize with restart after the UI change. Same-size comparison uses observed-tournaments.png/local-tournament-filters.png and artwork provenance is recorded in home-art/rea/tournament-navigation-regions.json.

PLO freezeouts: owner game selector accepts Pot-limit Omaha for 3/4/6-seat tournaments. Variant is persisted, validated and locked while occupied; the room uses four-card dealing, pot-limit caps and exactly-two-hole-card showdown across every new hand. PLO category now contains actual configured PLO events and details identify the game. Three-seat browser event and six-seat scheduled browser event both register through admin/lobby, fill computers, enforce pot controls, independently verify every observed settled payout and reach a persisted winner/prize; six-seat run conserves 600 chips and 150 credits. Unit suites cover both capacities and Hold’em regressions; owner API rejects unsupported variants and occupied changes. Found/fixed empty-room settings caching: an owner edit refreshes format/capacity after all registrations withdraw, preserving chat. Live update kept existing hosted/Rush snapshots and added an empty six-player PLO practice freezeout. This local format does not prove native five-max PLO tournament presentation or branded-event parity.

Custom practice prizes: owner may configure positive whole-percentage shares totalling 100, no more paid places than seats; winner-takes-all remains default. Occupied events lock the shares. Exact BigInt allocation floors each amount and distributes remaining whole credits by fractional remainder then finishing place, preserving the entire pool. Results map finishing places to per-player credits and show every paid player. Scheduled starts require at least max(2, paid places) entrants, otherwise cancel with recorded simulated refunds. Pure tests cover safe limits and rounding; API tests reject malformed/unsupported shares and occupied edits. Hold’em/PLO three-/six-seat integrations preserve totals and prizes. Browser custom 3-player Hold’em pays 53/22 from 75 credits; scheduled 6-player PLO pays 75/45/30 from 150 credits, independently checks hand payouts and persists all results after restart. Default/scheduling regressions pass. Live update preserved hosted/Rush snapshots, then explicitly configured the empty six-player PLO practice event to 50/30/20. Native payout rules/operator UI are not claimed.

First-screen account/data correction: local identity is now detected from /local/me on every lobby URL, independent of the ?player flag. Signed-out views show Guest and a working local sign-in entry; signed-in direct URLs use the actual profile and account-bound table routes. Captured native name/balances, account notification count and online population are no longer presented as local state. Native avatar artwork remains a separate decorative layer. Removed the two synthetic Hold’em rows with fixed computer occupancy and the unrelated standalone-practice shortcut from the native catalog; real host tables, capacity, seats and links come from the API. Browser acceptance creates a profile through the login UI, reopens a direct URL, verifies only the actual hosted table is listed, takes an account-bound seat, logs out and verifies Guest/required-login behavior. Two-hand five-computer acceptance still passes payouts, 720 conserved chips and restart recovery. Standalone fixture code remains separate and is tested through an explicit fixture URL rather than the native list. Native profile features, full account/wallet behavior and exact visual parity remain incomplete.

My Game Profile/avatar identity: retained observed-avatar.png establishes the profile layout, locked nickname, first six named choices and Apply. Local profile now offers those captured choices plus the default avatar, validates authenticated same-origin updates, saves atomically and exposes only avatar identity publicly. Invalid IDs/anonymous/foreign-origin writes and failed persistence are covered; failure leaves the prior avatar intact. Hosted room, spectator, selected-table preview and Rush views read the account avatar independently of seat index. Avatar-only updates repaint portraits without rebuilding hand/action/animation elements. Browser selects an avatar with Apply/cancel, moves into seat 3, updates from another tab, verifies spectator receipt with private cards hidden, preserves the identical prepared bet/history, independently verifies a complete 1200-chip hand and recovers the same avatar/payout after server restart and login. My Page/Game Settings navigation works. Existing two-hand, owner, tournament and Rush regressions pass. Live activation verified byte-identical stored JSON; public appearance fields are the only snapshot change. Native full catalog/uploads/country/account services, default-image resolution and complete pixel parity remain open.

Durable local login: account store v1 persists only SHA-256 cookie digests and the existing 12-hour expiry, alongside accounts in one atomic commit. Registration/login acknowledge only after the account/session write succeeds; logout is durably revoked before clearing the cookie. Old plain account files load unchanged and migrate on a successful write without changing names, hashes or avatars. Unit tests cover cold reload, separate-session logout, exact expiry, raw-token absence/private permissions, invalid/duplicate session records, failed login/logout rollback and lossless migration. Cookie-name matching is now exact. Browser verifies avatar/seat/hand recovery and root launch resume after actual backend restart without entering the PIN again; Hold’em/PLO Rush retain all twelve existing login cookies through restart and conserve 2400 chips. Normal two-hand regression passes. Live activation preserves byte-identical stored files until next authenticated write. Pre-update memory-only sessions still require one sign-in after this update; new sessions persist across later restarts. Native desktop-wrapper cookie retention and full profile/wallet parity remain unverified.

Native launcher: installed PLAYGG Local.app (com.danslab.playgglocal.codex) explicitly connects this checkout to the existing 18770 owner backend. It starts the same local data directory only when unavailable, keeps the server running on window quit, and restricts navigation to the configured loopback origin. Actual AppKit/WKWebView acceptance registers an isolated account, verifies the persistent HttpOnly cookie, 1200×961 content viewport and three native window controls, exits, then cold-launches a second process and verifies automatic login and backend reuse. Both stages pass; temporary QA data/server are removed. Ad-hoc signature verifies. Existing GGPoker-PlayMoney.app and DanGG.ro.app are separate clients and were left unchanged. Wrapper gameplay, full native visual parity, window-control interaction and speaker/timing equivalence remain unverified.

Native WebKit gameplay acceptance: extended the isolated AppKit runner through owner table creation using the admin form, persistent local login, Home Hold’em, real hosted-table selection, account-bound seating, Fill empty seats with computers and UI Check/Call through a six-seat showdown. It waits for twelve revealed cards and a visible award. Independent Node ranking recalculates winners, odd-chip distribution, every final stack and 720-chip conservation from the captured hand. A second cold app process resumes login, reclaims the same account seat through Take a seat (window sessionStorage is intentionally not durable), and verifies the complete hand snapshot unchanged. Both native stages and the independent verifier pass. QA changes are confined to tools/playgg-mac-test.swift, tools/native-hand-step.js, tools/native-hand-verify.mjs and tools/test-mac-app.sh; no live account/table mutation. Native reference comparisons, contested sound/animation timing and complete control parity remain open.

Native rendered evidence correction: hidden WKWebView windows suspend reveal/award animation even when all card elements exist in the DOM. Native QA now presents its isolated window and captures the settled showdown after three seconds. Visual inspection confirms all six two-card hands visible, WIN on the winning seat and its credited stack; independent ranking/payout and cold-relaunch assertions still pass. Saved six 1200×961 actual WebKit captures (login, owner, Home, list, pre-seat table and showdown), approximately 3.1 MiB total, linked in home-review.html. Earlier hidden-window captures did not prove visible animation. Current original screenshot remains on Switch Chip Display; no new seated-original or timing-parity claim follows from this run. Other live apps/data were unchanged.

Seat-name legibility repair: the 1200×961 visible WebKit showdown exposed the current player's long nickname plus (you) wrapping, increasing plate height into the cards. Added a geometry assertion for all six card rows against their nameplate top; it failed at seat 0 before the change. Shared network renderer now wraps the name in a single-line ellipsized span with full accessible label and hover text, preserving stack/award children and seat identity. Same WebKit journey now passes nonoverlap, independent payouts and cold reconnect; refreshed screenshot visibly separates names/cards. Chrome two-hand five-computer regression passes independent split payouts, animation order, carried balances, reconnect/release and 720 chips. Existing static server serves this on reload without backend restart. This repairs local legibility; native seated nickname truncation and full pixel parity remain unverified.

Betting options connected: shipped English resource bytes establish Mouse Wheel, Scroll up to increase/decrease, and Always/Never confirm labels; source SHA-256 and extraction limitations are retained in home-art/rea/betting-settings-strings.json. REA inspect-artifact rejects that JSON format, so direct parsed labels are explicitly static evidence rather than a REA behavioral result. Full Game Settings now enables Betting options beside Switch Chip Display; draft selectors save only on OK and discard on Cancel/Escape. Shared table renderer reads the saved wheel direction and Enter-confirm preference at use time without rebuilding an in-progress bet. Always confirm remains the existing default; Never confirm validates the amount then sends the same legal raise action. Browser red test failed on missing page before implementation; final settings acceptance verifies Cancel, persistence, shared-tab behavior, reversed wheel adjustment and a real Enter wager with no dialog. Existing chip-screen geometry, live preference sync, preserved prepared bet and unchanged pre-submit history pass. Default two-hand five-computer acceptance passes reveal/WIN/payout order, independent payouts, 720 chips and reconnect/release. Static changes available on live reload; no backend restart. Native betting-page placement/defaults and exact semantics beyond these labels remain unverified; other Game Settings pages remain disabled.

Sound settings connected: native shipped English strings establish Sound, Master Volume, Table Sound and Mute labels (digested source/extraction in home-art/rea/sound-settings-strings.json; native layout and scaling remain unknown). Full Game Settings now enables Sound with a 0–100 local master volume and table mute, using the same draft/OK/Cancel/Escape rules as betting/chips. Table audio routes every decoded source through one GainNode; saved volume is applied when audio unlocks and storage events update the existing node without recreating the context. Invalid out-of-range/noninteger storage falls back to full volume. Assert-based fake-WebAudio test failed on the missing gain path before implementation, then passed saved gain, live gain, zero, invalid bounds, source routing and mute/unmute. Browser settings test verifies discard, OK, shared-tab mute and reload persistence; existing chip geometry, betting preferences and prepared-bet checks pass. Two-hand one-player/five-computer browser regression passes source playback instrumentation, reveal/WIN/award order, independent payout, 720 chips and reconnect/release. Live static assets serve on reload with backend PID unchanged. Actual speaker output, native volume response/timing and category-specific effects remain unverified; no native Sound-page pixel match claimed.

Original-client alternate input probe: agent-desktop 2.6 reports Accessibility and Screen Recording granted and identifies original window w-3152 / PID 57441 / bundle com.ggnetwork.desktop.launcher.live.ggpcom, 1200×961 at (570,30), accessible=false. Explicit focus-window returns APP_UNRESPONSIVE / AXWindows -25204 with unknown delivery; later inventory still says not focused. A bounded headed pointer probe did not establish focus of the original; the full desktop contained overlapping apps/Spotlight, so no settings/game actions were sent to it. Original single-window capture remains Switch Chip Display. Do not count this as a native interaction test or keep retrying unchanged coordinates. No real-money/credential action or process restart occurred.

Hotkey full page connected: shipped English labels/digest retained in home-art/rea/hotkey-settings-strings.json. Game Settings → Hotkey now exposes Use Hotkey, Display Hotkey on screen and the established fixed F1/F2/F3 plus Ctrl+1–4 mappings. Both choices remain drafts until OK and synchronize through existing storage preferences. Shared table adds visible key hints only when enablement and display are both on, including actual preset buttons; handlers still reject text-entry focus, open confirmation dialogs, repeat/composing events and unavailable turns. Browser missing-page red test failed before implementation; final settings test passes page activation, OK and live open-table preference/key labels alongside sound/betting/chip regressions. Existing two-hand five-computer test passes guarded shortcuts, independent payouts, 720 chips, animation order and reconnect/release. Native key reassignment, full Hotkey-page geometry, global shortcuts and Rush new-hand key remain unimplemented/unverified. Live static changes are available on reload without backend restart.

Six-session readiness race fixed: extending hosted acceptance to a second unequal-stack hand exposed sequential Ready clicks from the same settled snapshot being rejected by strict table-version checks. Ready now carries the completed hand number; stale table versions are accepted only for that exact still-completed hand, and duplicate Ready is idempotent. Wagers and other operations retain strict version checks; stale hand-number/replayed Ready cannot mark a later hand. Unit red test reproduced the rejection, then passed shared-snapshot readiness, duplicate stability, persistence midway, one next deal, old-hand replay and stale-wager rejection. Six isolated browser accounts now play the first fold/showdown and second uneven hand end to end: actual contributions [372,120,120,4,116,116], seat 3 folds its 4-chip blind, uncalled 252 returned, independently calculated payout [252,12,0,0,0,584], wealth conserved including explicit local practice rebuys, folded cards hidden from other players/spectator, and exact payouts/stacks recovered after backend restart. No-limit, pot and 3/6-seat tournament tests pass. Live rollout done only with no active hands, all persisted files byte-identical and nine hosted watch snapshots unchanged. Native next-hand readiness presentation and real-client showdown timing remain unverified.

Hosted autonomous turn enforcement: cash-room deadlines and computer moves previously advanced only from player state requests; scheduled-event ticks also returned before processing active hands/next deals. Hosted server timer now visits every configured table each 500 ms. Tick applies one overdue check/fold (AoF folds), otherwise one computer move, and starts a due tournament next hand while preserving private spectator views; empty nonscheduled cash tables are not created. Exact-clock red test reproduced missing timeout, then passed boundary/fold/free-check, durable reload and computer progression. Actual isolated server test sends zero HTTP requests and observes persisted human timeout plus computer actions, then restart retention. Two-hand five-computer UI acceptance and real scheduled-event HTTP winner/prize test pass. Live activation performed only with all hands ended; saved-file digests and nine hosted snapshots compare equal. 90-second cash timeout/500 ms scheduler are local policy; native timing remains unverified. Standalone nonhosted fixture rooms still rely on client requests. AppKit activation returned true for the original client but did not establish visible focus; no further original-app input was attempted.

Computer pacing independent of polling: the new autonomous timer exposed the old state-request path still advancing one computer per poll. Clock test reproduced three actions from 100 immediate reads. Both tick and state now require at least 500 ms since the persisted turn began (derived from existing deadline, so no extra scheduling/checkpoint state). Test verifies 100 reads do not advance, 499 ms is too early, and 500 ms advances after a cold room reload. Human timeout still takes priority. AoF and Hold’em/PLO 3/6-player computer tournaments now advance their test clocks explicitly; all pass, including custom prizes. Real zero-request server timer/restart and readiness tests pass. Full two-hand Chrome play passes independent payouts, reveal/WIN/award order, 720 chips, carried stacks, reconnect and release. Only full-hand browser waits expanded from 10 to 30 seconds to account for deliberate pacing; control waits remain unchanged. Activated live with no active hands; all saved files and nine hosted snapshots unchanged. Minimum 500 ms is local policy, not native timing equivalence.

Selected-table preview geometry restored: full-size retained pair-select.png provides a native 1200×957 selected-table reference (left half below the 34-pixel comparison label). The newer 270-pixel CSS override compressed the preview; restored the 371 layout-pixel height and replaced the repeated center table name/stakes with the actual local Buy-in/chip amount. Shipped assets/preview/tpt_0.png and standard-table-preview.png SHA-256 identities match. Browser geometry assertion failed at 270 before the change and passes at 371; complete two-hand test retains six dynamic seat names/avatars/stacks, independent payouts, 720 chips and restart/release. Fresh local-selected-table.png is exactly 1200×957. Added selectedTableComparison to home-review.html; browser --comparison validates loaded original/local sources and equal rendered frame dimensions, with selected-comparison.png inspected visually.
